\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

(54)

is an automorphism of the Hopf algebra.

The second type of automorphisms is defined for
Hopf algebras given by relations of the form (
40
)–(
47
) and (
49
)–(
52
).

Lemma 5.19
.

There is a unique automorphism
\psi
of the Hopf algebra
A_{R,\omega,\omega^{*}}
or
A_{R,\omega}

acting as the identity on
\xi^{\pm}(u)
,
\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u)
and such that

\psi(T^{j}_{i}(u))=\xi_{2}^{+}(u-\lambda)T^{j}_{i}(u-2\lambda),\quad\psi((T^{*})^{-i}_{-j}(u))=\xi_{2}^{-}(u-2\lambda)(T^{*})^{-i}_{-j}(u-2\lambda),

where the second formula applies to the general linear case and, in the orthogonal case,

\xi^{+}_{2}(u)=\xi^{-}(u-\lambda)\xi^{+}(u).

Proof.

We need to check that
\psi
respect the relations. The non-trivial checks are for the relations
(
44
)–(
47
) in the general linear case and (
51
), (
52
) in the
orthogonal case.
For example in the relation (
44
)

\omega^{(23)}T^{(1)}(u+\lambda)T^{*(2)}(u)=\omega^{(12)}\xi_{2}^{+}(u),

the left-hand side is sent by
\psi
to

\displaystyle\xi_{2}^{+}(u)\xi_{2}^{-}(u-2\lambda)\omega^{(23)}T^{(1)}(u-\lambda)T^{*(2)}(u-2\lambda)

\displaystyle=\xi_{2}^{+}(u)\xi_{2}^{-}(u-2\lambda)\omega^{(12)}\xi_{2}^{+}(u-2\lambda)

\displaystyle=\xi_{2}^{+}(u)\omega^{(12)}.

The other cases are similar.

∎

Theorem 5.20
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be of type
A,B,C,D
.
For the restricted elliptic quantum algebra
E_{\ell}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
, the square of the antipode is

S^{2}=D_{\gamma}\circ\psi,

where
\gamma
is as in (
54
) with
g(a)=G_{a}
, see (
24
).

To prove this it is sufficient to apply
S^{2}
on the generators by using the definitions (
5.2
),
(
53
). One finds

S^{2}(T^{j}_{i}(u))=\xi_{2}^{+}(u-\lambda)\frac{G_{s(j)}G_{t(i)}}{G_{t(j)}G_{s(i)}}T^{j}_{i}(u-2\lambda),

and, in the general linear case,

S^{2}((T^{*})^{-i}_{-j}(u))=\xi_{2}^{-}(u-2\lambda)\frac{G_{s(j)}G_{t(i)}}{G_{t(j)}G_{s(i)}}(T^{*})^{-i}_{-j}(u-2\lambda).

Finally, we have (as in the case of Yangians and quantum affine algebras)
the shift by
z\in\mathbb{C}
of the spectral parameter for Hopf algebras
A_{R,\omega,\omega^{*}}
,
A_{R,\omega}
.

Since the structure constants are functions of the difference
u_{1}-u_{2}
, the maps on generators

\tau_{z}(T^{j}_{i}(u))=T^{j}_{i}(u-z),\quad\tau_{z}((T^{*})^{-i}_{-j}(u))=(T^{*})^{-i}_{-j}(u-z)\quad\tau_{z}(\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u))=\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u-z)

in the general linear case and

\tau_{z}(T^{j}_{i}(u))=T^{j}_{i}(u-z),\quad\tau_{z}((T^{*})^{-i}_{-j}(u))=(T^{*})^{-i}_{-j}(u-z)\quad\tau_{z}(\xi^{\pm}(u))=\xi^{\pm}(u-z)

in the orthogonal case extend to automorphisms of the Hopf algebra.

6.
Representations of the restricted quantum groups

We consider the setting described in the Section
4.2
. The
\pi
-graded vector spaces
V^{\textrm{RSOS}}
and
(V^{*})^{\textrm{RSOS}}
together with the restrictions of
R
-matrices and their rotations, given in Sections
4.1
and
4.3
, on these spaces provide the main example of the restricted quantum groups
E_{\ell}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
associated with Lie algebras
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
,
\mathfrak{so}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}
, see Definitions
5.16
,
5.17
.

6.1.
Vector representations

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
. The quantum group

E_{\ell}(A_{n-1})
has a
representation
V=(V^{\textrm{RSOS}},\sigma_{V})
on the
\pi
-graded vector space
V^{\textrm{RSOS}}
given by

\displaystyle\sigma_{V}(T_{i}^{j}(u))=(\check{R}_{VV})_{i}^{j}(u),\quad\sigma_{V}((T^{*})_{-i}^{-j}(u))=(\check{R}_{V^{*}V})_{-i}^{-j}(u),

\displaystyle\sigma_{V}(\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u))=\rho_{2}(u)^{\pm 1},

with
\check{R}_{VV}(u)
,
\check{R}_{V^{*}V}(u)
and
\rho_{2}(u)
given by (
23
), (
29
) and (
35
),
and the operators
(\check{R}_{VV})_{i}^{j}(u)
and
(\check{R}_{V^{*}V})_{-i}^{-j}(u)
defined as

\displaystyle(\check{R}_{VV})_{i}^{j}(u)e_{k}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l,\\
\varepsilon_{i}+\varepsilon_{k}=\varepsilon_{l}+\varepsilon_{j}\end{subarray}}(\check{R}_{VV})_{i,k}^{l,j}(u)e_{l},

\displaystyle(\check{R}_{V^{*}V})_{-i}^{-j}(u)e_{k}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l,\\
-\varepsilon_{i}+\varepsilon_{k}=\varepsilon_{l}-\varepsilon_{j}\end{subarray}}(\check{R}_{V^{*}V})_{-i,k}^{l,-j}(u)e_{l}.

Proof.

This is the result of Lemmas
4.2
,
4.3
, and
4.5
,
4.6
. ∎

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
The
R
-matrix operators
\check{R}_{i}^{j}(u)
defined as
\check{R}_{i}^{j}(u)e_{k}=\sum_{l}\check{R}_{i,k}^{l,j}(u)e_{l}
, where
\check{R}(u)
is given in (
22
), together with
\rho(u)
, given in (
34
), define a representation
V=(V^{\textrm{RSOS}},\sigma_{V})
of the restricted quantum group
E_{\ell}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
on the
\pi
-graded vector space
V^{\textrm{RSOS}}
for
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n}
,
C_{n}
and
D_{n}
:

\displaystyle\sigma_{V}(T_{i}^{j}(u))=\check{R}_{i}^{j}(u),\qquad\sigma_{V}(\xi^{\pm}(u))=\rho(u)^{\pm 1}.

Proof.

The statement follows directly from Lemmas

4.2
and
4.4
.
∎

Let us call
V
the vector representation of the restricted quantum group
E_{\ell}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
for
\mathfrak{g}
of type
A,B,C,D
.

Proposition 6.3
.

In the case of
B_{n}^{(1)},D_{n}^{(1)}
the vector representation representation splits as a direct
sum of two submodules

V=V^{+}\oplus V^{-}

where
V^{+}
is the direct sum of the homogeneous components of
V

of degree
(a,\epsilon_{i})
with
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and

V^{-}
is the direct sum of the homogeneous components of
V

of degree
(a,\epsilon_{i})
with
a\in(\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z})^{n}
.

Proof.

Let
P_{V}\subset P
be the sublattice spanned by the weights of the
vector representation. It is generated by
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{n}
for

A_{n-1},B_{n},C_{n},D_{n}
. For
A_{n},C_{n}
,

P_{V}=P
. For
B_{n}
and
D_{n}
,
P_{V}=\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and

P=\{a\in\mathbb{(}\frac{1}{2}Z)^{n}\,|\,a_{i}-a_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}\}
, see
Section
4.2
. In these cases
P_{V}
is the kernel of the map

P\mapsto\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
,
a\mapsto 2a_{1}\operatorname{mod}2
and
is thus a subgroup of index two. The set of objects of
\pi
is the union
of the two orbits
\pi_{0}^{\pm}
of
P_{V}
on
P
, given by weights with integer
and half-integer coordinates respectively. Let
\pi^{\pm}
be the corresponding full
subgroupoids of
\pi
. The generators
T^{i}_{j}(u)
with non-trivial image by
\sigma_{V}
are labeled by commutative squares in either
\pi^{+}
or
\pi^{-}
. So they
preserve
V^{+}
and
V^{-}
.
∎

As in the case of Yangians and quantum affine algebras we can twist a representation by a
shift automorphism, see Section
5.4
, to obtain new representations. For
z\in\mathbb{C}
let

\tau_{z}
be the automorphism acting on generators as
T^{i}_{j}(u)\mapsto T^{i}_{j}(u-z)
,

\xi^{\pm}(u)\mapsto\xi^{\pm}(u-z)
,
\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u)\mapsto\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u-z)
.
Then for any representation
W=(W,\sigma_{W})
of
E_{\ell}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
,

\sigma_{W}\circ\tau_{z}
is also a representation on the same underlying vector space
W
.
We denote it by
W(z)
. Since
\tau_{z+w}=\tau_{z}\circ\tau_{w}
,
W(z)(w)=W(z+w)
.

Definition 6.4
.

The representation
V(z)=(V^{\textrm{RSOS}},\sigma_{V}\circ\tau_{z})
of

E_{\ell}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
is called the

vector representation with spectral parameter
z
.

6.2.
Scalar representations and duality

Let
\mathcal{O}=\mathbb{C}[[z,p/z]]
, with
z=e^{2\pi iu},p=e^{2\pi i\tau}
be the ring of formal Fourier series
(see Section
2.5
). For each invertible element
f\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
of
\mathcal{O}
we have a representation
(\mathbb{C}_{f},\sigma_{f})
with underlying vector space with non-trivial weight spaces

(\mathbb{C}_{f})_{\mathrm{id}_{a}}=\mathbb{C},\quad a\in\pi_{0}.

In the orthogonal case
\mathfrak{g}=B_{n},C_{n},D_{n}
, the action of generators is

\sigma_{f}(T_{j}^{i}(u))=f(u)\delta_{ij},\quad\sigma_{f}(\xi^{\pm}(u))=f(u)^{\pm 1}f(u+\lambda)^{\pm 1}.

In the general linear case
\mathfrak{g}=A_{n}
, it is

\displaystyle\sigma_{f}(T_{j}^{i}(u))

\displaystyle=f(u)\delta_{ij},\quad\sigma_{f}(T^{*})_{{}_{j}}^{-i}(u))=f(u-\lambda)^{-1}\delta_{ij},

\displaystyle\sigma_{f}(\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u))

\displaystyle=\frac{f(u+\lambda)^{\pm 1}}{f(u-\lambda)^{\pm 1}}.

Lemma 6.5
.

These formulas define a representation of the restricted elliptic quantum groups
E_{\ell}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
. For
f=1
we obtain the trivial representation (given by the co-unit) and for any
f,g\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
,

\mathbb{C}_{f}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{g}\cong\mathbb{C}_{fg}.

Proof.

The proof is a verification of the relations, see Definitions
5.2
,
5.12
.
∎

Proposition 6.6
.

(i)

Let
W
be a representation of
E_{\ell}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
, such that
\xi_{2}(u)
acts by a scalar
\rho_{2}(u)\in\mathcal{O}
. Then for any
z\in\mathbb{C}
,

W(z)^{**}\cong W(z+2\lambda)\otimes\mathbb{C}_{f},\quad f(u)=\rho_{2}(u-z-\lambda).

(ii)

In the
B_{n}^{(1)},C_{n}^{(1)},D_{n}^{(1)}
cases, the vector representation obeys

V(z)\cong V(z-\lambda)^{*}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{f},\quad f(u)=\rho(u-z).

Proof.

(i)

The representation
W(z)^{**}
has the same underlying
\pi
-graded vector space
W

as
W(z)
but the action
of the algebra is twisted by the automorphism
S^{2}
. By Theorem
5.20
, the map

\phi\colon W_{\mu}\ni w\mapsto\frac{G_{a}}{G_{b}}w\otimes 1\in(W\otimes\mathbb{C}_{f})_{\mu}=W_{\mu}\otimes(\mathbb{C}_{f})_{\mathrm{id}_{b}},\quad\mu\in\pi(a,b),

with
f(u)=\rho_{2}(u-z-\lambda)

is an isomorphism
W(z)^{**}\to W(z+2\lambda)\otimes\mathbb{C}_{f}
.

(ii)

The relations (
58
) and (
57
) prove the identity

\displaystyle\omega\circ\sigma_{V(z)\otimes V(z-\lambda)}(T_{i}^{j}(u))=\omega\circ\sum_{k}\check{R}_{i}^{k}(u-z)\otimes\check{R}_{k}^{j}(u-z+\lambda)=\rho(u-z)\omega,

which together with

\displaystyle\omega\circ\sigma_{V(z)\otimes V(z-\lambda)}(\xi^{\pm}(u))=\rho(u-z)^{\pm 1}\rho(u-z+\lambda)^{\pm 1}\omega=\sigma_{f}(\xi^{\pm}(u))\omega

shows that the map
\omega
is an isomorphism
\colon V(z)\otimes V(z-\lambda)\to\mathbb{C}_{f}
for
f(u)=\rho(u-z)
.

∎

6.3.
A category of representations with elliptic coefficients

Out of the vector representation we can construct a subcategory of the monoidal
category of modules over our elliptic quantum algebras
E_{\ell}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
.
Namely we can take subquotients of

V(z_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes V(z_{r})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{f}

for any complex numbers
z_{1},\dots,z_{r}
and formal Fourier series
f
converging to an elliptic function. In the
A_{n}
case we can also replace
some factors
V(z_{i})
by
V^{*}(z_{i})
. This gives a rigid monoidal category
with a forgetful functor to
\pi
-graded vector spaces, and for any pairs

U,W
of objects we have a morphism
\check{R}_{UW}(z)\colon U(z)\otimes W\to W\otimes U(z)
for generic
z
; these morphisms
are solutions of Yang–Baxter equations
[
17
]
.

A priori on these representation the generators
T(u)
act by formal Fourier series (
39
). But
since these actions are polynomials in the matrix elements of generators
T(u)
for
the vector representation, which are meromorphic functions, the power series converge to meromorphic functions
of
u\in\mathbb{C}
and
\tau
in the upper half-plane. Moreover they are
doubly periodic:

Proposition 6.7
.

Let
q=e^{\frac{\pi i}{L}}
and
\tilde{\pi}
the set of arrows
(a,\epsilon_{i})

of
\pi
appearing as degrees in the vector representation of

E_{\ell}(\mathfrak{g}^{(1)})
. Let
\chi\colon\tilde{\pi}\to\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
be
the map
(a,\epsilon_{i})\mapsto a_{i}
. Then
the action of
T^{i}_{j}(u)
and
\xi^{\pm}(u)
,
\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u)
for
on
V(z_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes V(z_{r})
obeys

\displaystyle T^{i}_{j}(u+L)

\displaystyle=T^{i}_{j}(u),\quad T^{i}_{j}(u+L\tau)=q^{2(r+\chi(i)-\chi(j))}T^{i}_{j}(u),

\displaystyle\xi^{\pm}(u+L)

\displaystyle=\xi^{\pm}(u),\quad\xi^{\pm}(u+L\tau)=q^{\pm 4r}\xi^{\pm}(u),\quad\text{for $\mathfrak{g}=B_{n},C_{n},D_{n}$,}

\displaystyle\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u+L)

\displaystyle=\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u),\quad\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u+L\tau)=\xi_{2}^{\pm}(u),\quad\text{for $\mathfrak{g}=A_{n}$.}

In particular
T^{i}_{j}(u)
,
\xi^{\pm}(u)
are elliptic functions
of
u
with periods
L
and
2L^{2}\tau
.

Proof.

This is easily checked for
r=1
using the transformation properties

\frac{[x+L-a]}{[x+L-b]}=\frac{[x-a]}{[x-b]},\qquad\frac{[x+L\tau-a]}{[x+L\tau-b]}=e^{\frac{2\pi i}{L}(a-b)}\frac{[x+a]}{[x+b])}.

The general case follows by induction in
r
.
∎

Conjecturally, these properties hold for all finite dimensional representations.

In certain cases, we can require the central elements to act by 1:

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}=C_{n}^{(1)},D_{n}^{(1)}
. Let
L
be a period of the function
v\mapsto[v]
and
\lambda
the crossing parameter of the algebra. If
\lambda
and
L
are rationally related, i.e.
\frac{-\lambda}{L}=\frac{p}{q}
for
p,q\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\operatorname{gcd}(p,q)=1
, and
q
is odd, there exists a solution
\rho^{\prime}(u)
of the following equation

\rho^{\prime}(u)\rho^{\prime}(u+\lambda)=\rho(u),

(55)

where
\rho(u)
is given in (
57
), and it is

\rho^{\prime}(u)=\prod_{i=0}^{\frac{q-1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{[u+2i\lambda-1][u+(2i+1)\lambda]}{[u+2i\lambda][u+(2i+1)\lambda+1]}}\prod_{j=0}^{\frac{q-1}{2}-1}\sqrt{\frac{[u+(2j+1)\lambda][u+2(j+1)\lambda+1]}{[u+(2j+1)\lambda-1][u+2(j+1)\lambda]}}.

Proof.

It is easy to check that

\rho^{\prime}(u)\rho^{\prime}(u+\lambda)=\sqrt{\frac{[u+q\lambda-1][u+(q+1)\lambda][u-1][u+\lambda]}{[u+q\lambda][u+(q+1)\lambda+1][u][u+\lambda+1]}}.

We have the condition
q\lambda=-pL
and we use the periodicity
[u-pL]=[u-p^{\prime}L]=[u]
to obtain the result.
∎

In these cases we can consider the restricted quantum group with central elements
\xi^{\pm}(u)=\eta(1)
. Its representation is given by the renormalization of the
R
-matrix,

\tilde{\check{R}}(u):=\frac{1}{\rho^{\prime}(u)}\check{R}(u).

Appendix A
General results in the unrestricted model

Proposition A.1

(Jimbo, Miwa, Okado
[
30
]
)
.

The Boltzmann weights satisfy the following properties:

•

Reflection symmetry:

\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}a&b\\
d&c\end{matrix}\Big|u\Big)=\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}a&d\\
b&c\end{matrix}\Big|u\Big),

(56)

•

Rotational symmetry (for
B_{n}^{(1)}
,
C_{n}^{(1)}
and
D_{n}^{(1)}
):

\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}b&a\\
g&c\end{matrix}\Big|u\Big)=\rho(-u)^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{G_{a}G_{g}}{G_{b}G_{c}}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}a&c\\
b&g\end{matrix}\Big|\lambda-u\Big),

(57)

with

\displaystyle\rho(u)=\frac{[u-1][\lambda+u]}{[u][1+\lambda+u]},

•

Inversion relation:

\sum_{g}\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}b&g\\
d&c\end{matrix}\Big|u\Big)\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}b&a\\
g&c\end{matrix}\Big|-u\Big)=\delta_{a,d},

(58)

•

Additional inversion relation for
A_{n-1}^{(1)}
:

\sum_{g}\sqrt{\frac{G_{a}G_{c}G_{g}^{2}}{G_{b}^{2}G_{d}^{2}}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}a&b\\
d&g\end{matrix}\Big|\lambda-u\Big)\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}c&d\\
b&g\end{matrix}\Big|\lambda+u\Big)=\delta_{a,c}\rho_{2}(u),

(59)

with

\displaystyle\rho_{2}(u)=\frac{[\lambda+u][\lambda-u]}{[1+\lambda+u][1+\lambda-u]},

•

Star-triangle relation:

\begin{split}\sum_{g}\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}f&g\\
e&d\end{matrix}\Big|u_{2}\Big)\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}b&c\\
g&d\end{matrix}\Big|u_{1}-u_{2}\Big)\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}a&b\\
f&g\end{matrix}\Big|u_{1}\Big)\\
=\sum_{g}\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}a&b\\
g&c\end{matrix}\Big|u_{2}\Big)\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}a&g\\
f&e\end{matrix}\Big|u_{1}-u_{2}\Big)\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}g&c\\
e&d\end{matrix}\Big|u_{1}\Big).\end{split}

(60)

Corollary A.2
.

The Boltzmann weights associated to the algebra
A_{n-1}^{(1)}
satisfy the relation

\begin{split}\sum_{g}\sqrt{\frac{G_{a}G_{c}G_{g}^{2}}{G_{b}^{2}G_{d}^{2}}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}g&b\\
d&a\end{matrix}\Big|\lambda+u\Big)\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}g&d\\
b&c\end{matrix}\Big|\lambda-u\Big)=\delta_{a,c}\rho_{2}(u).\end{split}

(61)

Proof.

We need to show

(1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda+u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda-u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}

\displaystyle\frac{G_{g}G_{a}}{G_{b}G_{d}}=\rho_{2}(u),

(2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda+u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda-u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}

\displaystyle\frac{G_{g}G_{a}}{G_{b}^{2}}

+\sum_{j\neq i}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda+u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda-u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}

\displaystyle\frac{G_{g^{\prime}}G_{a}}{G_{b}^{2}}=\rho_{2}(u),

(3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda+u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda-u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}

\displaystyle\frac{G_{g}\sqrt{G_{a}G_{c}}}{G_{b}^{2}}

+
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda+u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda-u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}

\displaystyle\frac{G_{g^{\prime}}\sqrt{G_{a}G_{c}}}{G_{b}^{2}}

+\sum_{k\neq i,j}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda+u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda-u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{j}}

\displaystyle\frac{G_{g^{\prime\prime}}\sqrt{G_{a}G_{c}}}{G_{b}^{2}}=0.

The first relation follows directly from the definition and the observation that

\displaystyle\frac{G_{g}G_{g+\varepsilon_{i}+\varepsilon_{j}}}{G_{g+\varepsilon_{i}}G_{g+\varepsilon_{j}}}=\frac{[g_{i}-g_{j}]^{2}}{[g_{i}-g_{j}-1][g_{i}-g_{j}+1]}.

The second and third relation come from the identity (
59
), in particular from expressions

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{[1]^{2}}{[\lambda+u+1][\lambda-u+1]}\frac{[\tilde{a}_{i}-\tilde{a}_{j}-\lambda-u][\tilde{a}_{i}-\tilde{a}_{j}-\lambda+u]}{[\tilde{a}_{i}-\tilde{a}_{j}]^{2}}\frac{G_{\tilde{a}}G_{\tilde{a}+\varepsilon_{i}+\varepsilon_{j}}}{G_{\tilde{a}+\varepsilon_{i}}G_{\tilde{a}+\varepsilon_{j}}}

\displaystyle=\rho_{2}(u)

and

\displaystyle\frac{[1]}{[1+\lambda+u]}\frac{[\tilde{b}_{j}-\tilde{b}_{i}-1-\lambda-u]}{[\tilde{b}_{j}-\tilde{b}_{i}-1]}G_{\tilde{b}+\varepsilon_{i}}+\frac{[1]}{[1+\lambda-u]}\frac{[\tilde{b}_{i}-\tilde{b}_{j}-1-\lambda+u]}{[\tilde{b}_{i}-\tilde{b}_{j}-1]}G_{\tilde{b}+\varepsilon_{j}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\neq i,j}\frac{[1]^{2}}{[1+\lambda+u][1+\lambda-u]}\frac{[\tilde{b}_{i}-\tilde{b}_{k}-1-\lambda+u]}{[\tilde{b}_{i}-\tilde{b}_{k}-1]}\frac{[\tilde{b}_{j}-\tilde{b}_{k}-1-\lambda-u]}{[\tilde{b}_{j}-\tilde{b}_{k}-1]}G_{\tilde{b}+\varepsilon_{k}}=0,

with substitutions
\tilde{a}=-g-\varepsilon_{i}-\varepsilon_{j}
and
\tilde{b}=-b
.
∎

Corollary A.3
.

The Boltzmann weights associated to the algebras
B_{n}^{(1)}
,
C_{n}^{(1)}
and
D_{n}^{(1)}
obey the identities

\sum_{g}\sqrt{\frac{G_{g}}{G_{c}}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}a&c\\
b&g\end{matrix}\Big|u+\lambda\Big)\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}b&g\\
d&c\end{matrix}\Big|u\Big)=\sqrt{\frac{G_{b}}{G_{a}}}\delta_{a,d}\rho(u),

(62)

and

\sum_{g}\sqrt{\frac{G_{g}}{G_{b}}}\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}b&d\\
g&c\end{matrix}\Big|u\Big)\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}g&c\\
b&a\end{matrix}\Big|u+\lambda\Big)=\sqrt{\frac{G_{c}}{G_{a}}}\delta_{a,d}\rho(u).

(63)

Proof.

The first relation is obtained by inserting the rotational symmetry relation (
57
) in the inversion relation (
58
). The second relation comes from the first one by applying the reflection symmetry (
56
) and the double rotation

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}a&b\\
c&g\end{matrix}\Big|u+\lambda\Big)=\mathcal{W}\Big(\begin{matrix}g&c\\
b&a\end{matrix}\Big|u+\lambda\Big).

∎
